Schema: `inventory_transactions.requires_review boolean DEFAULT false`, `inventory_transactions.job_id uuid REFERENCES jobs(id)`. Rewrite: `complete_job` no longer raises on insufficient stock — applications happen in the field and the DB must record reality; short-stock transactions tagged `requires_review = true` and surface in activity feed. Linked-prebook decrement: `quantity_prebooked` only drops when the job's `quote_section_id` matches an `inventory_holds.source_id`, fixing the leak where Customer B's job silently halved Customer A's prebook. |
| 249 | 20260430170000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 4 — Application Service Fees** — fixes codex audit #8. Schema: `jobs.application_service_id uuid REFERENCES application_services(id)` (parity with `blend_tickets.application_service_id` and `invoices.application_service_id`). New helper RPC: `compute_application_service_fee(p_service_id uuid, p_customer_id uuid, p_acres numeric, p_season int)` — single source of truth for fee math; lookup priority is `customer_application_rates` override → `application_services.default_rate_per_acre_cents` → 0. Returns jsonb `{ rate_per_acre_cents, total_fee_cents, cost_per_acre_cents, total_cost_cents, source, service_name }`. Existing inline fee blocks in `save_field_app_invoice` and `create_invoice_from_blend_ticket` continue to work; future cleanup can refactor them onto the helper. |
| 250 | 20260430180000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 5 — RLS Hardening** — fixes codex audit #10. Tightens 3 policies to close a privilege escalation vector: `jobs_update` is now admin/sales only (was: any applicator with auth.uid = applicator_id, which let applicators silently mutate price/customer/applicator_id via direct PostgREST); `job_applied_info_insert` and `job_applied_info_update` now require ownership (the auth user must be the applicator assigned to the linked job). `start_job` and `complete_job` are SECURITY DEFINER so applicator workflows continue to work through the RPCs. |
| 251 | 20260430190000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 7 — Codex re-review #1+#2+#3 hot fixes** — three bugs surfaced by the codex re-review of Phases 1–6. **#1 (P1)** added internal auth gates to `start_job` + `complete_job`: validate `auth.uid() = p_performed_by`, then check role (`is_admin()` OR `is_sales_rep()` OR (`is_applicator()` AND `v_job.applicator_id = auth.uid()`)). Closes the privilege-escalation hole that Phase 5's RLS could not (SECURITY DEFINER bypasses RLS). **#2 (P1)** changed `complete_job`'s linked-prebook lookup from `inventory_holds.source_id = jobs.quote_section_id` to `... = jobs.quote_id` — planned-program holds are keyed by quote_id (verified against migration `20260317100000:384,397`); the previous lookup never matched. **#3 (P2)** replaced the single-row hold UPDATE with an oldest-first loop that takes up to each row's `quantity`, preventing CHECK violations when multiple holds exist for the same quote+product. |
| 252 | 20260430200000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 8 — Codex re-review #4 hot fix** — `save_field_app_invoice` edit path now cancels and detaches orphan group child invoices when an admin edits a draft grouped invoice and the new derived customer list drops a previously-billed customer. Per Mason's call (Option B), orphans are marked `status = cancelled`, `invoice_group_id = NULL`, totals zeroed (consistent with the items/shares already wiped), and an `invoice_orphan_cancelled` activity_feed row records the audit trail. Posted/voided members remain protected by the existing edit lock. |
| 253 | 20260430210000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 9 — Sprint A1 auth gates on 3 field-app billing RPCs** — addresses P1 findings from money-inventory and security-permissions audits (2026-04-30). Adds `auth.uid()` gate + actor-mismatch reject + admin/sales role check to `save_field_app_invoice`, `create_invoice_from_blend_ticket`, and `post_invoice_group`. Pattern mirrors `save_quote` and Phase 7 `start_job`/`complete_job`. SECURITY DEFINER bypasses RLS, so internal auth checks are the only protection — previously these RPCs could be called by any authenticated user with a spoofed `p_performed_by`/`p_created_by`. |
| 254 | 20260430220000 | **Field App Workflow Phase 10 — Sprint A2 + Sprint B (folded)** — auth gates + invoice integrity rules on `save_invoice` and `create_invoice_from_order`. New RPC takes a `FOR UPDATE` lock on the Main Warehouse inventory row, recomputes today's free (= `available − prebooked − active holds where expires_at IS NULL OR ≥ CURRENT_DATE`) inside the transaction, and `RAISE EXCEPTION 'INSUFFICIENT_HOLD_INVENTORY'` if the requested qty would push it negative. Admin can override by passing `p_force=true` with a non-blank `p_force_reason` (mirrors PO over-receive override pattern from Phase 21 G2). Sales reps can create non-force holds; force-create is admin-only. Idempotent: keyed on `(idempotency_key, operation)`, 24-hour TTL, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Activity feed entry on every successful create with `WARNING:` prefix when forced. Frontend: `InventoryPage.tsx` `handleCreateHold` rewired to call the RPC; new `<ReasonModal>` mounted to capture force-reason when the server rejects with `INSUFFICIENT_HOLD_INVENTORY` and the user is admin. This migration adds a due_date check after the lift: `'paid' && balance > 0 && due_date < CURRENT_DATE` -> `'overdue'`, otherwise `'posted'`. Each policy was dropped and recreated with the same action, roles, permissive flag, and predicate — only the auth.uid() calls changed. | 287 | 20260511060000 | **Perf sweep #2 — consolidate overlapping permissive RLS policies** (Supabase advisor `multiple_permissive_policies`, 33 WARN → 23 unique overlap groups across 16 tables). Multiple permissive policies covering the same (role, action) cause Postgres to evaluate each predicate per row and OR them together. Consolidation merges each overlap group into one permissive policy whose predicate is the OR of the originals — same union of access, one evaluation per row instead of N. Patterns handled: (A) `_admin_all FOR ALL` + role-specific INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/SELECT (`deliveries`, `delivery_items`, `delivery_photos`, `delivery_remainders`, `receiving_photos`, `receiving_records`) — split the FOR ALL into action-specific policies merged with the role policies; (B) `_admin_all FOR ALL` + open `_select` qual=true (`application_services`, `customer_application_rates`, `quote_pdf_templates`, `quote_templates`) — drop the admin_all SELECT side (the open SELECT covers it), keep admin INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE narrow; (C) admin SELECT + sales_rep SELECT (`purchase_orders`, `purchase_order_items`) — merge with `is_admin() OR is_sales_rep()`; (D) admin SELECT + user-own SELECT (`document_processing_log`, `commissions`) — merge with admin OR ownership predicate; (E) redundant admin policies (`failed_notifications` had a SELECT policy redundant with a FOR ALL one — drop the SELECT). **Sensitive-table change:** `rate_limit_log` had a permissive `qual=false` "Deny all direct access" policy that was a no-op (permissive false OR'd with permissive admin = admin; access semantics unchanged). Replaced with a **RESTRICTIVE** `FOR ALL admin_only` policy as defense-in-depth — if a future migration ever adds a permissive policy by mistake, the RESTRICTIVE still blocks non-admins. All rewritten policies also use `(SELECT auth.uid())` wrapping so this migration closes the 8 residual auth_rls_initplan findings. Verification asserts old policies are gone, 39 new policies exist, and the RESTRICTIVE policy is the correct permissive flag. |
